### The Conductor Developer
Source: Martin Fowler
Published: 2026-10-25 02:41 UTC
URL: https://martinfowler.com/rachels-ramblings/conductor-developer.html
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R5 · P5 · baseline

TL;DR Why I think software development is starting to feel a little more like conducting an orchestra. There’s a shift happening in software development that I don’t think we’re talking about clearly enough. For the last couple of years we’ve framed AI as a productivity tool. How much faster can it write code? How many more features can we ship? How much cheaper can we build software? I think that’s the wrong question, but I understand why. The first thing AI became good at was writing code, so naturally that’s where we focused. As AI got better at coding, I expected the bottlenecks to move through the software delivery lifecycle: from coding to design and specification, architecture, then verification. And they have. We spent a lot of time at the most recent FOSE event discussing how we ensure good design, quality and resilience while agents increasingly write the code. That’s a topic for another ramble. A few months ago, though, I realised I was looking at the wrong bottleneck. I kept assuming it would simply move to the next phase of software delivery. I was wrong.

### Fragments: August 4
Source: Martin Fowler
Published: 2026-10-25 02:41 UTC
URL: https://martinfowler.com/fragments/2026-08-04.html
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R5 · P5 · baseline

There’s been a fair bit of publicity of the Open AI “rogue agent” that hacked into Hugging Face . This prompted Anthropic to check what their models were up to and, to my complete lack of surprise, discovered three incidents where models had gained unauthorized access to data in other organizations. Simon Wilison concluded : It’s abundantly clear now that running evals of cyberattack potential in models is a spectacularly risky business. Every AI lab needs to pay attention to this. Keeping a close eye on what’s happening in those sandboxes is crucial It strikes me that this is akin to a virus escaping from a laboratory. It makes clear that the model builders are not putting sufficient controls in place to prevent these lab escapes. They are morally responsible for any consequences of this, and that should extend to legal liability too. The bigger concern however is that this same kind of thing can happen with any organization running open-weight models. Lots of labs playing around with dangerous tools and little idea how to contain them.

### Fragments: August 18
Source: Martin Fowler
Published: 2026-10-25 02:41 UTC
URL: https://martinfowler.com/fragments/2026-08-18.html
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R5 · P5 · baseline

Part of the reason why I’m at Thoughtworks is because I’d like to see a software development organization founded on technical excellence as an example for the rest of the industry. The trouble is that I have little aptitude or inclination for the hard work of building such an organization. So I rely on working with people who are prepared to actually put the effort in. A key partner in all of this is Rachel Laycock , who is the global CTO of Thoughtworks. Not just is she far better than me at running a technology organization, she’s also a keen observer and connector of ideas. I’ve been urging her to write these down, even if her busy schedule makes it difficult for her to compose them into something substantial. Happily she’s starting writing “Rachel’s Ramblings” Fast, imperfect, thinking out loud. Naming ideas early rather than waiting until they’re fully formed. Because the reality is, most of what I do day to day isn’t answering known questions. It’s spotting patterns and asking questions we haven’t quite figured out yet.

### 333 Tor Exit Nodes Hijacked To Intercept SSH Traffic And Retrieve Plaintext Email
Source: SoylentNews
Published: 2026-10-25 02:41 UTC
URL: https://soylentnews.org/breakingnews/article.pl?sid=26/10/22/1248234&from=rss
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

https://arstechnica.com/information-technology/2026/10/300-tor-exit-nodes-hijacked-to-intercept-ssh-traffic-and-retrieve-plaintext-email/ Unknown hackers hijacked more than 300 Tor exit nodes and reconfigured them to favor unencrypted email traffic and redirect SSH connections through servers they controlled, a researcher reported Tuesday. The hijacking began in late August, when according to Tor volunteers , the attacker used a valid password that had been reused to gain access to a Tor management server. The attacker then installed Ebury , a backdoor written for Linux that was discovered 12 years ago. Then the attacker connected from the management server to a second Tor server, called Switzerland2, through SSH, using an RSA key that, for unknown reasons, investigators couldn't find among Switzerland2's authorized keys. With administrative control of Switzerland2, the attackers installed Ebury there as well. Finally, the attacker reconfigured settings on two exit nodes that Switzerland2 hosted.

### Hello Python 3.15, goodbye 3.10, and now I can use 3.11
Source: Simon Willison's Weblog
Published: 2026-10-25 02:41 UTC
URL: https://simonwillison.net/2026/Oct/12/python-3-15/
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Python 3.15 is here ! Here's the essential What's new in Python 3.15 guide, this year curated by 3.14 and 3.15 release manager Hugo van Kemenade. As someone who builds open source software that works in every supported Python version, I get to say goodbye to Python 3.10 and start embracing features from Python 3.11. First, some highlights from Python 3.15. Explicit lazy imports I'm most excited about Explicit lazy imports from PEP 810 . These let you import modules like so: lazy import json lazy from pathlib import Path The module will then be imported the first time those symbols are used in the code that follows. My LLM CLI tool implements plugins. I had to recommend manual lazy imports in the plugin author documentation, because simply installing plugins that used heavy dependencies such as sentence_transformers could add multiple seconds to every invocation of the llm command. Lazy imports directly address this startup cost. Unpacking in comprehensions The new [*sublist for sublist in lists] unpacking syntax is neat.

### Quoting Geoffrey Huntley

If the agent isn't using my diagnostics library, I could introspect and update AGENTS.md, but there's still a delta, and it'll drift. Instead, I write a pre-commit hook that checks the git diff for direct calls to observability modules that bypass my diagnostics module. Boom: fail the commit, inject a prompt telling the agent to use the diagnostics library. All of a sudden it's ten out of ten. — Geoffrey Huntley , nobody should type code by hand anymore. here's what's left. ### [$] The end of __get_user() and __put_user()
Source: LWN.net
Published: 2026-10-22 16:25 UTC
URL: https://lwn.net/Articles/1102061/
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Linus Torvalds rarely writes significant amounts of kernel code these days, so the merging of a 48-part patch series from him is an unusual event. This series, destined for the 7.4 kernel release, performs some long-delayed kernel cleanup by removing a couple of the primitives used to access user-space memory from the kernel. What was once a best practice has long been superseded, but it can take time for such changes to work their way through a body of code this large.

### Beyond API Compatibility: Building a Tool to Measure the Blast Radius of Library Changes
Source: Netflix TechBlog
Published: 2026-10-23 18:32 UTC
URL: https://netflixtechblog.com/beyond-api-compatibility-building-a-tool-to-measure-the-blast-radius-of-library-changes-9474f2fd4f90?source=rss----2615bd06b42e---4
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R5 · P5 · baseline

By Roberto Perez Alcolea , Java Platform Team Quasar connects a proposed API change with observed references in consumer repositories, giving the producer evidence for a release decision At Netflix, shared Java libraries support applications developed across a large, independently evolving repository ecosystem. A library change begins in one repository, but its impact can reach many others. Producers can see the API diff in front of them. What they often cannot see is who still depends on the symbols that changed. The need for that visibility grows as coding agents take on more of the software development lifecycle. An agent can accelerate a local edit, but without cross-repository context it cannot judge downstream risk or help plan consumer migrations. The evidence a producer uses to assess a change can also guide the agent working on it. Consider a common case: removing a deprecated API. Deprecated entry points expand the surface producers must test, document, and support, and they can constrain how a library evolves.

### Single-tenant Titus: VM-level isolation on a multi-tenant container platform
Source: Netflix TechBlog
Published: 2026-10-22 22:19 UTC
URL: https://netflixtechblog.com/single-tenant-titus-vm-level-isolation-on-a-multi-tenant-container-platform-6d8b1909d163?source=rss----2615bd06b42e---4
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R5 · P5 · baseline

Samartha Chandrashekar & Nick Parker You click play on a Friday night, and in the second that follows, dozens of services answer in milliseconds. Most of them run on Titus, our internal container platform. Titus is, in essence, a Kubernetes-based container hosting platform: we keep both the control plane and the data plane (the container hosts that actually run containers) patched and up to date, so application teams get a consistent and reliable container experience. They hand us a container, and we run it, with no host, cluster, or kernel to own. Titus launches millions of containers every week across tens of thousands of EC2 instances in multiple AWS regions. It is multi-tenant by default: many containers from many teams share each host. That density is what makes container compute efficient, and it is why Titus has been the default home for so much of Netflix’s compute for years. For most workloads, sharing a host works well. For a few, it does not, and those few include some of the most demanding real-time systems we run.

### Introducing new self-learning context enrichment in Knowledge Catalog
Source: Google Cloud Blog

URL: https://cloud.google.com/blog/products/data-analytics/knowledge-catalog-self-learning-context-enrichment-for-ai-agents/
Source meta: institutional · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Today, we’re launching self-learning context enrichment (preview) and a reimagined Knowledge Studio experience (preview) in Knowledge Catalog , Google Cloud’s context engine for agents. Building on Knowledge Catalog's existing Gemini-powered enrichment, self-learning enrichment analyzes agent and user interactions to find gaps and keep your business context accurate and current. Knowledge Studio is the new command center for context engineers and data stewards to explore their data estate conversationally, navigate a visual context graph, and review proposed changes to the context graph before they are published to their users and agents. An agent is only as good as the organization-wide definitions, rules, and relationships it reasons over — the context, as it were. That’s why curating context has become one of the most important disciplines in enterprise agentic capabilities. Today, most of that work happens by hand. Context engineers write prompts and mappings agent by agent, while data stewards try to manually keep definitions current across thousands of assets.

### Inside Agent Compute Engine: Building the compute foundation for the agentic era
Source: Google Cloud Blog
Published: 2026-10-22 13:00 UTC
URL: https://cloud.google.com/blog/topics/ai-infrastructure/agent-compute-engine-deep-dive/
Source meta: institutional · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Every decade or so, a shift in software forces us to rethink computer architecture from first principles. After the first boom of web-scale traffic, Google pioneered warehouse-scale computing and Linux containers on Borg. This reshaped how we run internal workloads and ultimately sparked the modern cloud-native ecosystem through Kubernetes. A decade later, when deep learning models outgrew their compute and power needs, we went back to the drawing board and created Tensor Processing Units (TPUs). And as cloud security demanded stronger, hardware-rooted guarantees, we built Titanium , a custom silicon and Infrastructure Processing Unit (IPU) architecture that offloads I/O virtualization, storage, and cryptographic trust from the host CPU. Today, we are in the middle of the next foundational shift: the transition to agentic AI that goes beyond answering questions and to taking action. Across Google, AI agents are writing and executing code, running tools, driving browsers, orchestrating enterprise workflows, and iterating on software repositories.

### Introducing Agent Compute Engine and expanded agent governance
Source: Google Cloud Blog
Published: 2026-10-22 04:00 UTC
URL: https://cloud.google.com/blog/topics/ai-infrastructure/introducing-agent-compute-engine-and-expanded-agent-governance/
Source meta: institutional · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Blog authors: Nirav Mehta, VP Product Management; Michael Bachman, VP/GM; Kevin Hutchins, VP Product Management Scaling Gemini to planet scale taught us firsthand that securely running autonomous agents is fundamentally an infrastructure challenge. The shift from single-turn prompting to interactive agents requires a new paradigm of execution and control. To support massive concurrent agent sessions, we engineered new compute and governance solutions for Gemini to help solve these challenges. Today at AGNTCon + MCPCon, we are delivering these capabilities to customers by launching Agent Compute Engine , a purpose-built compute service that already powers Gemini as an agent for work tasks. It will be available in preview in the coming months, providing hardware-isolated runtime sandbox security. Additionally, we are extending Gemini for business’ agent governance capabilities to support agents wherever they operate. Together, these capabilities deliver the control you need to run, govern, and scale your agents.

### BigQuery ObjectRef now GA: Build multimodal data platforms for conversational analytics entirely in SQL
Source: Google Cloud Blog
Published: 2026-10-21 16:00 UTC
URL: https://cloud.google.com/blog/products/data-analytics/analyze-unstructured-data-in-bigquery-with-objectref/
Source meta: institutional · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

For data science, data engineering, and machine learning teams, unlocking the value of unstructured data, such as images, videos, audio, and documents, has historically meant managing siloed data pipelines for structured and unstructured data, and for analytics and AI/ML use cases. Even when files remain in Cloud Storage, running inference requires teams to orchestrate external machine learning workloads, only to face the complex, brittle challenge of stitching those structured inference outputs back into the data warehouse for analysis. This post-hoc data-stitching introduces significant operational overhead and database misalignment. ObjectRef and multimodal data analytics in BigQuery are now in General Availability (GA) , establishing BigQuery as a comprehensive, multimodal agentic data platform. With ObjectRef, BigQuery natively bridges the gap between Cloud Storage and your analytical engine. You can represent unstructured data as native ObjectRef columns directly alongside your traditional relational columns.

Authors: Divya Gadde , Ko-Jen Hsiao , Dhaval Patel and Moumita Bhattacharya The motivation for building the Netflix Foundation Model for personalization was laid out in this previous blog post. The Netflix homepage is powered by several specialized models and it requires significant time and resources to maintain and continue innovating on these individual models. Therefore, we decided to centralize member preference learning and have one powerful model learned from comprehensive user interaction histories and content data at a large scale and have this model distribute its learnings to other models. The previous two blog posts( 1 , 2 ) focus on the development of the Foundation Model and here we talk about how we integrate the Foundation Model into various applications. We found that there is extensive literature focused on the training and inference of large-scale transformer-based models. However, there remains a significant gap in practical guidance and research on how to effectively and efficiently integrate large models into existing production systems.

This blog post aims to illuminate these hurdles and explain how an innovative open-source metric platform became instrumental in overcoming some of them. We’ll showcase the critical role that DataJunction played in streamlining our data workflows and enhancing our ability to derive meaningful insights from our vast datasets. Metrics are the foundation of data-driven decision-making, but at many organizations, they are scattered, inconsistently defined, and difficult to discover or trust. At Netflix, this challenge first became apparent in our experimentation platform, where reliable metrics are essential for interpreting test results. ### Measuring the Impact of Personalized Recommendations
Source: Netflix TechBlog
Published: 2026-10-14 19:57 UTC

By Kevin Zielnicki, Guy Aridor, Aurélien Bibaut, Allen Tran, Winston Chou, and Nathan Kallus Introduction Personalization is everywhere on the internet. It shapes the products we discover, the stories we read, the music we hear, and the movies and TV shows we watch. At Netflix, personalization has been central to the member experience for decades — from the Netflix Prize to our modern recommender system that helps members navigate a large and constantly changing catalog. But personalization is not only an algorithmic problem, it is also a measurement problem. How do we know how much value personalization creates? More precisely, how do we separate the appeal of a title from the value created by recommending that title to the right member at the right time? In a new article in the International Journal of Industrial Organization — which we are also presenting at this year’s ACM Conference on Economics & Computation (EC ‘26) — we develop a model of member behavior, title choice, and personalized recommendations.

### Why Is My Kubernetes Job Still Pending? Tracing Kueue Admission with Headlamp
Source: Kubernetes Blog
Published: 2026-10-14 18:35 UTC
URL: https://kubernetes.io/blog/2026/10/14/tracing-kueue-admission-with-headlamp/
Source meta: institutional · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

You submit a Kubernetes Job and wait for its Pods to appear. A minute passes. Then another. The Job is still suspended, there are no application logs, and there may not even be a Pod to inspect. In a cluster managed by Kueue , this can be completely normal. Kueue may still be deciding whether the Job can use the requested resources. The useful question is not only “Why is the Pod pending?” It is first “Has Kueue admitted the Workload?” The Kueue plugin for Headlamp brings that investigation into the Headlamp UI. Instead of reconstructing the admission path from several commands and large YAML objects, you can begin with the Workload, read its condition message, and follow links through the LocalQueue, ClusterQueue, ResourceFlavor, and Cohort. This article follows that Headlamp-first path. The command line appears only in a short optional section for reproducing the example. Before you begin You need a Kubernetes cluster with Kueue installed , a Headlamp installation , and the 0.1.0-alpha Kueue plugin .

### The Shift to cgroup v2 in Kubernetes: What You Need to Know
Source: Kubernetes Blog
Published: 2026-10-06 18:00 UTC
URL: https://kubernetes.io/blog/2026/10/06/kubernetes-cgroups-v2-shift/
Source meta: institutional · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

In Linux, cgroups (control groups) are a kernel feature used for managing system resources. Kubernetes uses cgroups to allocate resources like CPU and memory to containers, ensuring that applications run smoothly without interfering with each other. With the release of Kubernetes v1.31, support for v1 cgroup management moved into maintenance mode . Support for v2 cgroup management has been stable since Kubernetes v1.25. Compared with cgroup v1, cgroup v2 provides a single unified hierarchy, a more consistent interface, and a stronger foundation for resource isolation and modern resource-management features. Deprecation of cgroup v1 Kubernetes has deprecated cgroup v1 . Starting with Kubernetes v1.35, failCgroupV1 defaults to true , so the kubelet does not start on a cgroup v1 node by default. Administrators can temporarily set failCgroupV1: false in the kubelet configuration file , but removal will follow the Kubernetes deprecation policy . Further removal work is tracked in KEP-5573: Remove cgroup v1 support . If you are still on a release older than v1.

### Scaling Kubernetes Workloads with Node Swap
Source: Kubernetes Blog
Published: 2026-10-05 18:00 UTC
URL: https://kubernetes.io/blog/2026/10/05/scaling-kubernetes-workloads-with-node-swap/
Source meta: institutional · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Memory is often the first hard limit a Kubernetes cluster hits. Nodes run out of RAM long before they run out of CPU, and the new wave of agentic AI workloads makes this worse. These workloads demand large memory footprints to start up and run untrusted code, then sit idle waiting for the next prompt. That idle but resident memory is expensive, and it caps how many pods a node can hold. This is where swap helps. Kubernetes support for running nodes with swap enabled reached General Availability in v1.34, and by backing that swap with fast NVMe solid state drives (SSDs), a node can page out dormant memory and pack in far more pods. This post explains how we benchmarked that approach across three workloads, including CI/CD kernel builds, sandboxed headless browsers, and isolated Python runtimes; we found density gains of up to 3×, often with little or no latency cost.

### Spotlight on SIG Apps
Source: Kubernetes Blog
Published: 2026-09-22 18:00 UTC
URL: https://kubernetes.io/blog/2026/09/22/sig-apps-spotlight/
Source meta: institutional · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

As Kubernetes adoption has grown, the conversation has shifted beyond running containers to managing increasingly complex application lifecycles. Modern platforms support stateless web services, stateful databases, batch processing, AI workloads, and platform services. At the same time, they must remain reliable during upgrades, scaling events, and infrastructure failures. Every Kubernetes user relies on SIG Apps, whether they realize it or not. Deployments, StatefulSets, DaemonSets, Jobs, and CronJobs form the foundation of how applications are deployed, updated, scaled, and operated across the Kubernetes ecosystem. SIG Apps is focused on improving workload resilience, refining application lifecycle management, and addressing the operational challenges that emerge when applications encounter node failures, rollout disruptions, and increasingly complex infrastructure environments.

### Kubernetes v1.37: Tracking When a PersistentVolumeClaim Was Last Used (Beta)
Source: Kubernetes Blog
Published: 2026-09-21 18:30 UTC
URL: https://kubernetes.io/blog/2026/09/21/kubernetes-v1-37-pvc-last-used-time/
Source meta: institutional · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Kubernetes v1.37 promotes the PersistentVolumeClaimUnusedSinceTime feature gate to Beta (enabled by default). With this feature, the PersistentVolumeClaim (PVC) protection controller adds an Unused condition to each PVC, telling you whether any running pod currently references it — no custom tooling or cross-referencing required. For the API definition of PVC conditions, see the PersistentVolumeClaim API reference . Read on to learn how the Unused condition works and how to use it. Why track PVC usage? In large-scale Kubernetes clusters, it is common for users to create PVCs and then delete the associated pods without cleaning up the storage, because Kubernetes does not automatically delete PVCs when their pods are removed (to protect against accidental data loss). Over time, these orphaned PVCs may accumulate, silently consuming storage capacity and driving up cloud costs. Before Kubernetes v1.37, it was easy to identify an unused PersistentVolume, but much harder to determine whether a PVC was still being used.

### Kubernetes v1.37: Hardening Container Storage with Bind Mount Options and EmptyDir Permissions

URL: https://kubernetes.io/blog/2026/09/16/kubernetes-v1-37-hardening-container-storage/

Kubernetes v1.37 brings important storage security features: emptyDir permission modes and bind mount options. They help application programmers and security professionals implement rigorous security policies, for example, prohibiting deletion of files across containers or execution of arbitrary binaries from writable volumes, directly in Kubernetes without any complicated circumvention. Linux storage and permission fundamentals Before diving into the new Kubernetes features, let us briefly review the low-level Linux security mechanisms that make them possible. Bind mount flags When Linux mounts or remounts a directory, Virtual File System (VFS) flags control what actions are permitted on that filesystem: noexec : Do not permit direct execution of any binaries on the mounted filesystem. nosuid : Do not allow set-user-identifier or set-group-identifier bits to take effect. nodev : Do not interpret character or block special devices on the file system. [ ETH Zurich Robotics Systems Lab ] Humans effortlessly react to the world; they adeptly return a tennis shot, catch a falling cup, step aside from a passing cart, or pass a box tossed during truck unloading. Such visual reactive whole-body control remains challenging for humanoids. ### The Robot I Didn’t Expect to Build
Source: IEEE Spectrum Robotics
Published: 2026-10-19 13:00 UTC
URL: https://spectrum.ieee.org/agility-robotics-digit-5-redesign
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Agility Robotics , the company I cofounded out of my robotics lab at Oregon State University in 2015, has just launched Digit 5 . It’s designed to be the first humanoid robot that can safely and economically do useful work at scale, allowing us to bring tens of thousands of humanoid workers into the real world. Engineered to lift 23-kilogram bins onto high shelves and with a battery that can run for 90 minutes on a 9-minute charge, Digit 5’s goal is to handle heavy warehouse tasks shift after shift, filling the need for labor in jobs that are too physically stressful or too dull for humans to want to do. Digit 5 is what Agility has been working toward for over a decade. But the robot is heavy and bulky, lacking the graceful natural motion of its predecessors, and it’s farther than I thought possible from the robot I dreamed of building when I started this company. To deliver a useful commercial product, Agility has taken a path that is very different from what I first imagined.

### Hera Mission Prepares for Follow-Up Asteroid Imaging
Source: IEEE Spectrum Aerospace
Published: 2026-10-18 13:00 UTC
URL: https://spectrum.ieee.org/esa-hera-dimorphos-rendezvous
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

In one month, the European Space Agency’s Hera mission will make history. Moving at 12 kilometers per second relative to Earth, the mission is about to rendezvous with the binary Dimorphos-Didymos asteroid pair. NASA’s Double Asteroid Redirection Test (DART) mission slammed into Dimorphos in 2022, and now Hera will provide a follow-up view of the mission’s resulting handiwork. The European Space Agency (ESA) recently released a thought-provoking look at the perspectives of Hera and what an astronaut might see from the surface of the asteroids as the mission speeds by. But what would it be like to hitch a ride aboard Hera? Launched on a SpaceX Falcon 9 rocket from Cape Canaveral, Fla., on 7 October 2024, Hera is ESA’s first Space Safety Programme-class mission. The spacecraft took just over two years to reach its destination. Even now, the pair of asteroids is little more than a few pixels across in Hera’s view. The mission imaged Didymos in July 2026.

### Q&A: Mars Perseverance Rover Sets a Distance Record
Source: IEEE Spectrum Robotics
Published: 2026-10-13 15:02 UTC
URL: https://spectrum.ieee.org/mars-rover-distance-record
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

On 11 October , the Perseverance rover completed a drive that brought its total distance traveled on Mars to 28.10 miles (45.22 kilometers). This is a new record for Mars rovers, enabled in large part by Perseverance ’s ability to drive with much more autonomy than its predecessors. Over the past five years, Perseverance ’s autonomy software has been steadily upgraded, and to learn more about how the record was broken and what the future holds for the rover, we spoke with Mark Maimone , the Perseverance Robotic Operations Deputy Team Chief at NASA’s Jet Propulsion Laboratory, who designed and developed the self-driving software for Mars rovers Spirit, Opportunity, Curiosity, and Perseverance. Before we talk about Perseverance, let’s pour one out for the previous record holder, Opportunity , which landed on Mars in January 2004 for a three-month mission and ended its epic journey 15 years later in February 2019.

### NASA Wants More Than 70 Moon Landings in the Next Decade—But How?
Source: IEEE Spectrum Aerospace
Published: 2026-10-12 15:27 UTC
URL: https://spectrum.ieee.org/nasa-moon-base-landings
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

At Firefly Aerospace in Texas, engineers have been doing what NASA’s chief, Jared Isaacman, likes to call “the near-impossible.” The Firefly team has been assembling Blue Ghost Mission 2 , a robotic spacecraft intended to land on the far side of the moon in 2027. No U.S. spacecraft has ever gone there. Not very many have landed on the near side, either, for that matter. Blue Ghost 2 has thousands of potential failure points, and there is almost no room for error. Two ships sent by a competitor, Houston-based Intuitive Machines , reached the moon’s near side in 2024 and 2025 but fell over on landing and returned little useful data. In 2024, another mission, Peregrine Mission One , by Pittsburgh-based Astrobotic Technology , missed the moon entirely because a single helium valve wouldn’t seat properly. In summary: Moon landings are supremely hard. So far, since Project Apollo ended in 1972, U.S. spacecraft have landed intact exactly…once.

### Lunar Mission Will Explore Old Lava Tubes as Potential Habitats
Source: IEEE Spectrum Aerospace
Published: 2026-10-11 13:00 UTC
URL: https://spectrum.ieee.org/nasa-moon-mission-gimli-artemis
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

The chief aim of NASA’s Artemis Program is to create a “sustained program of lunar exploration and development.” Beyond returning astronauts to the moon for the first time since the Apollo era , NASA and other space agencies hope to build the infrastructure that will enable regular, long-duration missions to the lunar surface. In addition to targeting the South Pole-Aitken Basin , NASA is eyeing lunar caves as a potential site for habitats and other lunar facilities. Meanwhile, space agencies need to send large amounts of cargo and robotic explorers to the surface. This includes the Geophysical Instruments for Marius Lunar pit Investigation (GIMLI), a Planetary Science Institute –led proposal that will investigate a “skylight,” or a hole in an old lunar lava tube, to see if a large cave system extends beyond it . Such a location could provide a safe place for astronauts to operate on the surface in the future. The mission team will be led by Than Putzig , an associate director and senior scientist at PSI.

### This Disembodied Hand Is All the Robot You Need
Source: IEEE Spectrum Robotics
Published: 2026-10-08 12:00 UTC
URL: https://spectrum.ieee.org/walking-robotic-hand
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

A hand attached to the end of an arm is a given for most humans, but what if robots didn’t have to play by the same rules as we do? Researchers from ETH Zurich have turned an off-the-shelf robotic hand into a completely self-contained robot that walks on its fingertips while using those same fingers to manipulate its environment. Besides making the perfect Halloween accessory for anyone planning to dress up as a member of the Addams Family—who were famously accompanied by a disembodied walking hand called Thing —this robotic hand could make it possible to operate controls and handle objects in places a traditional robot arm can’t reach, the researchers say. Soft Robotics Lab, ETH Zurich/YouTube Amirhossein Kazemipour , a Ph.D. candidate in the Soft Robotics Lab at ETH Zurich who led the project, believes this hand could prove useful in contexts like fiddly maintenance tasks in industrial settings, or in search and rescue, where a robot could detach its hand to explore confined spaces too tight for the rest of its body.

### Ukraine’s Air Defenses Have No Answer for Russia’s New Jet Drones
Source: IEEE Spectrum Aerospace
Published: 2026-09-30 11:00 UTC
URL: https://spectrum.ieee.org/jet-powered-shahed-drone
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Since the discovery of the first remnants of a jet-powered Shahed drone near Kyiv in June 2025, these upgraded kamikaze UAVs have become a nightmare for Ukrainian civilians. Described by some analysts as the most significant Russian drone innovation since the beginning of the war, these uncrewed aerial vehicles are outrunning the beleaguered nation’s innovative interceptor systems, forcing a pivot in technology development and air-defense strategies. Russia manufactures the Iran-designed long-range Shahed drones , under the name Geran at its Yelabuga factory in Tatarstan, some 800 kilometers east of Moscow. In a little over a year, Russian engineers have developed three iterations of the jet-powered killer drone— Geran-3 , 4, and 5. Geran-3 , although fitted with a Chinese-made Telefly JT80 turbojet engine that enabled it to reach speeds of up to 370 kilometers per hour, was not very maneuverable and for that reason not much more effective than the propeller-powered Geran-2 .

### Generative AI Gives Spacecraft the Autonomy Engineers Once Feared
Source: IEEE Spectrum Aerospace
Published: 2026-09-28 11:00 UTC
URL: https://spectrum.ieee.org/generative-ai-in-space-exploration
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Space was always supposed to be the final frontier of human exploration. It’s shaping up to be the final frontier for artificial intelligence too. Last December, NASA’s Jet Propulsion Laboratory used Anthropic’s Claude models to help plan two Mars drives for the Perseverance rover , with human planners checking and adjusting the route before upload. In May, NASA and IBM put a compressed AI model on the International Space Station and a satellite to identify things like floods and clouds from orbit, the first model of its kind demonstrated in space. And in July, astronauts on the ISS tested a large language model to see if it could help with questions on maintenance procedures . These experiments point to a larger shift in space engineering. For decades, engineers on Earth determined what a machine in space would do, and the machine would do exactly that. Now, researchers are testing whether nondeterministic systems like generative AI can give spacecraft more flexibility to interpret their surroundings, plan tasks, and one day make decisions for themselves.

### Spain’s First Astronaut, Pedro Duque, Named IEEE Honorary Member
Source: IEEE Spectrum Aerospace
Published: 2026-09-22 18:00 UTC
URL: https://spectrum.ieee.org/spain-astronaut-ieee-honorary-member
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Many youngsters fascinated by exploring outer space dream of becoming an astronaut, but few do. One who had the right stuff is Pedro Duque , who was Spain’s first astronaut. The aeronautics engineer flew aboard the space shuttle Discovery and the International Space Station . After retiring as an astronaut, he headed Spain’s Ministry of Science, Innovation, and Universities . Today he is the chairman of HispaSat , a Spanish satellite company. Pedro Duque Employer HispaSat in Madrid Title President and chairman of the board Member grade Honorary member Alma mater The Polytechnic University of Madrid Nearly 30 years after his first mission, Duque is still Spain’s most famous astronaut. Three public schools have been named after him, and he has received numerous awards. This year, the IEEE Board of Directors made him an IEEE honorary member for “contributions to space exploration, leadership in collaborative science and technology programs, and serving as a role model for younger generations.

### RoboParty RP1 (ROBOTO 01) – A full-stack open-source humanoid robot with up to 30 degrees of freedom
Source: CNX Software
Published: 2026-10-23 00:00 UTC
URL: https://www.cnx-software.com/2026/10/23/roboparty-rp1-roboto-01-a-full-stack-open-source-humanoid-robot-with-up-to-30-degrees-of-freedom/
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Shanghai-based RoboParty has introduced the RP1 (ROBOTO 01), an open-source humanoid robot designed for robotics research, embodied AI development, and motion-control experiments. The robot is available in two configurations: the standard RP1 with 24 active degrees of freedom (DoF) and the RP1 Pro with 30 DoF. The robot stands 1.35 meters (~4.43 feet) tall and is built around a Rockchip RK3588 processor, with the Pro model adding an Allwinner A733 processor and an NVIDIA Jetson Orin NX development computing module. Other features include in-house ROMOMO joint actuators with up to 160 N·m peak torque, interchangeable head modules, optional dexterous hands, and a 10Ah battery rated for up to three hours of operation. A separate 480MHz MCU handles real-time motion control, while a dual-voltage battery system supports hot-swapping without shutting down the robot. ### Why AI Can’t Replace Engineering Teams

Published: 2026-10-22 18:02 UTC

Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

This article is crossposted from IEEE Spectrum ’s careers newsletter. Sign up now to get insider tips, expert advice, and practical strategies, written i n partnership with tech career development company Parsity and delivered to your inbox for free! If you’re an engineer, you may feel like your skills aren’t commanding the premium they once did. Many engineers have faced layoffs or restructuring. But even for those of us who still have jobs, something corrosive is happening. You may have heard some version of this: “ChatGPT answered that in five seconds. Why can’t you?” Or, “I made this app in a weekend. Why is our team taking so long?” I worked on a team where leadership said things like this directly to engineers. This was in 2024 and 2025, when the AI craze was in full swing and Claude and ChatGPT adoption was rising . Demo-Driven Development Generative AI (and the marketing behind it) gives people the illusion that the work is simpler than it is.

### Why Engineers Should Brave Capitol Hill
Source: IEEE Spectrum
Published: 2026-10-22 13:00 UTC
URL: https://spectrum.ieee.org/engineers-lobbying-congress
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

When San Antonio–based software engineer Nikhil Jathar first went to Washington, D.C., to advocate for federal support for technology education and research funding, he was in awe of the opportunity. It was spring of 2025, not long after Jathar settled in the United States from India, when he got an invitation to join the annual Congressional Visits Day organized by IEEE-USA . “My first reaction was, ‘Wow, I get to visit Congress,’ ” says Jathar, CTO of AvanSaber , a business software company he cofounded in 2014. At the time, he did not have a specific lobbying position on behalf of the company. But he was intrigued by the invitation—which goes out to all U.S. IEEE members—and as he read more about congressional visits, his motivation deepened. He realized: “I need to try this. I want to see where it goes.” What’s Your Ask? Congressional staffers expect clarity from advocates. Be clear on what you plan to ask for when you arrive. Here are some common options: Cosponsor (or oppose) an existing bill or amendment.

### Manfred “Fred” J. Schindler Is 2027 IEEE President-Elect
Source: IEEE Spectrum
Published: 2026-10-21 18:00 UTC
URL: https://spectrum.ieee.org/schindler-2027-ieee-president-elect
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

IEEE Life Fellow Manfred “Fred” J. Schindler has been elected as the 2027 IEEE president-elect . He will begin serving as president on 1 January 2028. Schindler, who was nominated by the IEEE Board of Directors , received 16,421 votes in the election. Senior Member David Alan Koehler received 13,874 votes. Schindler’s Top 3 Goals as President-Elect Strengthen IEEE’s connection with industry by coordinating events across geography, industry, and technology to revitalize professional activities, especially ones for young professionals. Champion the improvement of IEEE’s digital infrastructure to eliminate manual processes and modernize interfaces. Prioritize the advancement of technology to benefit future generations by expanding and supporting IEEE initiatives and programs. At press time, the results were unofficial until the IEEE Board of Directors accepts the IEEE Teller’s Committee report in November. Schindler, an expert in microwave semiconductor technology, is an independent consultant supporting clients with technical expertise, due diligence, and project management.

### How Wikipedia’s Editors Keep the Online Encyclopedia Human-Written
Source: IEEE Spectrum
Published: 2026-10-21 13:00 UTC
URL: https://spectrum.ieee.org/wikipedia-wikiproject-ai-writing-cleanup
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Ellia Lebleu is a founding member of the WikiProject AI Cleanup , a volunteer effort launched in 2023 to find and remove AI-generated material from Wikipedia that breaks the website’s guidelines. When the cleanup began, AI writing was often betrayed by conspicuous clichés. Today, the project’s volunteers are developing tools to detect subtler forms of machine-generated content and debating AI’s role in the world’s most widely used encyclopedia. When did you realize Wikipedia had a problem with AI-generated material? Ellia Lebleu: It was around December 2023. A few other editors and I were chatting on Wikipedia’s Discord server, and we had all seen very strange articles with the same odd characteristics. They repeated phrases like “stands as a testament” or “rich cultural heritage.” They sometimes had conclusion sections, or sections called “Why is this important?” That was very typical of ChatGPT in 2023 and early 2024 but very odd for Wikipedia articles—and very easy to spot. Then I decided, let’s create an official project on Wikipedia so more people can deal with this.

### Measuring Human Cryptanalysis Effort
Source: Schneier on Security
Published: 2026-10-25 02:41 UTC
URL: https://www.schneier.com/blog/archives/2026/10/measuring-human-cryptanalysis-effort.html
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R5 · P5 · baseline

Matthew Green has a new site that tries to estimate the number of cryptanalyst-hours that have been spent analyzing different public-key algorithms: Abstract: We estimate how much public cryptanalysis each public-key assumption has received, using the literature itself as the instrument. From roughly sixteen thousand candidate papers we classify cryptanalytic work on ECDLP, structured and plain lattices, syndrome decoding and their comparators, then convert papers into estimated research-allocation years through a person-level author map. These publication-based estimates may substantially overstate actual time spent. Lattices have overtaken elliptic curves in this cumulative measure only recently; we express that history as inference budgets under stated productivity assumptions. The study was conducted by AI models: Claude Fable 5.1 and Claude Opus 5.5 built the pipeline, ran the analysis and wrote this page, with contributions from GPT-6 Astra. Matthew Green set the questions, made the key methodological decisions and oversaw the work...

### Backdooring Open-Weight AI Models
Source: Schneier on Security
Published: 2026-10-25 02:41 UTC
URL: https://www.schneier.com/blog/archives/2026/10/backdooring-open-weight-ai-models.html
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R5 · P5 · baseline

This is a problem: Any open model whose weights have been edited can carry a backdoor, whether it’s a task-specific fine-tune, a merged adapter or an abliterated build. We have abliterated models in the title because it’s the most popular reason people download modified weights without verifying what’s inside. Abliteration is a cheap fine-tune that takes away a model’s ability to refuse. You find the direction in the weights that carries “no” and subtract it. The model then answers things a safety-tuned version would turn down. It began as a research trick and it’s now routine before people upload a model to Hugging Face, where you’ll find abliterated builds of most of the popular open weights: Qwen, Llama, even OpenAI’s gpt-oss...

### Training AIs to Solve CAPTCHAs
Source: Schneier on Security
Published: 2026-10-25 02:41 UTC
URL: https://www.schneier.com/blog/archives/2026/10/training-ais-to-solve-captchas.html
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R5 · P5 · baseline

Interesting research : Abstract : Vision-language models (VLMs) can solve visual CAPTCHAs without task-specific training, but the agents built on them approach every challenge from scratch. For such an agent, the hundredth instance of a familiar puzzle costs as much time and compute as the first. Specialized detectors invert the trade-off, answering in milliseconds but only for categories they were trained on. Neither improves with exposure. We study what changes when a solver improves with use. Our system pairs a fine-tuned YOLOv8 detector with an open-weight VLM behind a confidence-based router, and runs entirely from screenshots and operating-system input events, with no browser automation or DOM access. It reaches 85.4% overall and 84.2% macro accuracy across 16 classes, exceeding either component alone. Every answer VLM produces also serves as a training label, so the detector absorbs categories it was never trained for, typically after one or two encounters and without human annotation. The same loop also repairs it.

### The Vulnerability Apocalypse and Why We Should Fund Open Source
Source: Electronic Frontier Foundation
Published: 2026-10-23 22:36 UTC
URL: https://www.eff.org/deeplinks/2026/10/vulnerability-apocalypse-and-why-we-should-fund-open-source
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

If you use the internet at all, you may have noticed that a large percentage of sites suddenly went down earlier this week — and all your nerdiest friends were talking about "The Vulnerability Apocalypse” and this thing called PHP that powers nearly 70% of the websites on the internet . You may find yourself wondering: what is PHP, what just happened, what is the vulnerability apocalypse, and what do I need to do? To find out what you can personally do read the first blog post in this series! What Is PHP? PHP is a programming language used mainly to create interactive websites. Facebook, Wordpress, Wikipedia, Etsy, and other major sites all use or were originally built on PHP. It is a long-established mainstay of web development. But because it is open-source, maintenance of these critical systems depends on a small number of volunteers working on this in their free time. The disaster scenario that happened this past week is the perfect example of why we need more money and people supporting open-source projects. Corporations should be contributing both.

### Computer Security Is About to Get a Lot Worse, Here’s What You Can Do
Source: Electronic Frontier Foundation
Published: 2026-10-23 22:36 UTC
URL: https://www.eff.org/deeplinks/2026/10/computer-security-about-get-lot-worse-heres-what-you-can-do
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

It is a commonly held truth among computer geeks that there is no such thing as bug free software, or a secure computer. Any piece of software that anyone actually uses will have a security vulnerability somewhere in the code . The more complex that software is, the more likely there will be a vulnerability. Fortunately for everyone, finding severe vulnerabilities was generally considered an expensive and difficult task requiring years of training and deep knowledge of how computers and software worked. Until recently there was a steady background hum of vulnerabilities being found and patched, with major vulnerabilities that could cripple the entire internet only happening every few years. Now that is no longer the case. You may have noticed your nerdiest friends freaking out about the “Vulnerability Apocalypse” this week and waving their arms in panic about something called PHP. You may have also noticed that some of your favorite websites were down for a bit.

### Two States Can Now Restrict Social Media Access—Here’s What To Do Next
Source: Electronic Frontier Foundation
Published: 2026-10-23 18:19 UTC
URL: https://www.eff.org/deeplinks/2026/10/two-states-can-now-restrict-social-media-access-heres-what-do-next
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Last week, two federal appeals courts issued opinions that will allow Florida and Georgia to start enforcing laws that will age-gate and block young people from accessing social media. The laws do not adequately respect minors’ rights to play, speak, and learn online. More fundamentally, these laws don’t help young people stay safe online—a goal that lawmakers should pursue. Age-Gated? Email EFF at agegates@eff.org . Because of the new laws, some young people will lose their ability to access lawful information, find their online communities, and even engage with school board members. Others will need parental permission to stay online. And adults will face increased demands to prove their age, which will put their privacy at increased risk simply to continue to use the services they already use. We opposed these laws because they threaten the free expression and privacy of both minors and adults.

### Canadians Are Running Out Of Time To Oppose The Dangerous C-22 Surveillance Bill
Source: Electronic Frontier Foundation
Published: 2026-10-22 22:10 UTC
URL: https://www.eff.org/deeplinks/2026/10/canadians-are-running-out-time-oppose-dangerous-c-22-surveillance-bill
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Canada’s Parliament is debating a massive expansion of surveillance powers that will threaten the privacy and security of millions of people. Bill C-22, also called the Lawful Access Act, has already passed the House of Commons, and is now before the Canadian Senate. Canadians still have an opportunity to speak out and stop this dangerous legislation. The Canadian Civil Liberties Association (CCLA) is urging all Canadians to sign its petition opposing the bill. (The petition on CCLA’s website is governed by CCLA’s privacy policy.) Take action Tell the canadian senate to stop C-22 As we wrote earlier this year , Bill C-22 gives the government new powers to force digital services—including telecom companies, messaging apps, and more—to help with government surveillance. Worse, it allows Canada’s Minister of Public Safety to order companies to create a backdoor to their services for law enforcement, as long as it doesn’t lead to a “systemic insecurity.” That’s a clear recipe for weakening the encryption we all rely on.

### Introducing Our New Surveillance Self-Defense Guide: Online Privacy for Parents and Youth
Source: Electronic Frontier Foundation
Published: 2026-10-21 23:07 UTC
URL: https://www.eff.org/deeplinks/2026/10/introducing-our-new-surveillance-self-defense-guide-online-privacy-parents-and
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

The internet is a vast and confusing place no matter your age. Trying to help youth engage with it safely and privately is a complicated topic that touches on many different skillsets and ideas. But just because it’s complicated doesn’t mean it’s impossible. We’ve launched our Online Privacy for Parents and Youth Surveillance Self-Defense guide in hopes to provide a framework for finding solutions that work for you. EFF rolls out this crucial resource as state and local governments consider ill-advised laws that cut young people off from essential information and experiences. This particularly harms vulnerable youth and marginalized groups who often find safety in supportive online communities they can't access offline. Such laws also entail age gating, which compromises the privacy of all internet users and stifles young users’ ability to learn, grow , and express themselves . Young people have unique needs and those needs change over time.

### Interoperability vs. Security: Why not both?
Source: Electronic Frontier Foundation
Published: 2026-10-21 22:39 UTC
URL: https://www.eff.org/deeplinks/2026/10/interoperability-vs-security-why-not-both
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

An open internet where people can freely build, share, and remix each other's work is a powerful engine of grassroots innovation we’ve all benefited from. In recent decades, however, corporations have taken control of an increasing share of this work, giving them a greater ability to stifle challengers and limit our choices. When regulators address this by mandating that tech giants play fair by requiring interoperability, the gatekeepers respond with a tired refrain: Playing fair puts users’ security at risk. Unfortunately for these trillion-dollar companies, this gotcha isn’t the end of the conversation. Regulators can ensure that meaningful choice and security thrive. Lawmakers have been catching up, recognizing that the best way to rein in big tech is to require these oligopolies to open up their platforms to independent developers. As we explained six years ago , mandating interoperability helps prevent the misuse of people’s data and enables more privacy-respecting alternatives. Tech giants, in contrast, have been arguing that our data is safer when they have exclusive control.

### Out Now: Low-tech Magazine Volume IV
Source: Low-Tech Magazine
Published: 2026-09-24 00:00 UTC
URL: https://solar.lowtechmagazine.com/2026/09/out-now-low-tech-magazine-volume-iv/
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R3 · P4 · culture_probe

Image: Low-tech Magazine Volume IV (2022-2026). Photo: Juliette Demaret. Low-tech Magazine presents the fourth volume in the chronological book series. It contains twenty articles that were published online between 2022 and 2026. Three earlier volumes collect articles written and published online between 2007 and 2021, which are also bundled in the “ Compressed Edition ”. This new volume is a bit different from the earlier books: eight of the articles are DIY guides or manuals, and we have grouped them together in the second part of the book. This volume covers the period that Marie Verdeil joined Low-tech Magazine, which partly shifted the focus from writing articles to building prototypes inspired by historical research. These prototypes also led us to conduct workshops in universities and participate in museum exhibitions. As is the case with all of our books, the articles have been edited and improved compared to the original web versions. Some articles gained extra or better images.

### Rediscovering the Handcart
Source: Low-Tech Magazine
Published: 2026-04-22 00:00 UTC
URL: https://solar.lowtechmagazine.com/2026/04/rediscovering-the-handcart/
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R3 · P4 · culture_probe

Image: The handcart, equipped with a sail. Photo by Kris De Decker. The human-powered handcart is the oldest of vehicles, and it will likely be the last one around in the future. Of all vehicles, it’s the cheapest and least complex to build and use. It offers a large advantage over carrying a load on your back or dragging it over the ground - the even older concept of the sled. On the other hand, the handcart is cheaper and easier to use than the animal-powered cart. Oxen and donkeys eat more than humans, and they have their own will, which can work against the driver. Like any other wheeled vehicle, the handcart requires roads to drive on. This infrastructure has not always been available anywhere or at any time in history. For example, in medieval Europe, porters and pack animals were more common than handcarts because of poor roads. 1 In the West, the handcart only reached its heyday during the first decades of the Industrial Revolution, when it connected fast-growing cities to train stations and harbors.

### Low-tech Magazine: The Uncompressed Book Series
Source: Low-Tech Magazine
Published: 2026-03-30 00:00 UTC
URL: https://solar.lowtechmagazine.com/2026/03/low-tech-magazine-the-uncompressed-book-series/
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R3 · P4 · culture_probe

Image: The redesigned and revised chronological book edition. Photo: Marie Verdeil. Between 2019 and 2021, Low-tech Magazine published three books containing selections of articles from the website, spanning 14 years (2007-2021). In 2024, we launched the “compressed edition” , which squeezes the article catalog of the three-volume book series into just one book of 620 pages. We did this by switching to a smaller font size, downsizing most images, and opting for a two-column layout. I rewrote some articles, especially older ones, resulting not only in fewer pages but also in better articles. This revised edition of the original, “uncompressed” series is based on the edits made for the compressed edition. However, we increased the font and image sizes and returned to a one-column layout for improved reading comfort. The book design is a collaborative effort by Laia Comellas, Marie Verdeil, and Johanna Gratzer. Marie Verdeil made the covers, Vaiva Vinskaitė did the typesetting. Image: The redesigned and revised chronological book edition. Photo: Marie Verdeil.

URL: https://therestartproject.org/right-to-repair/repair-reuse-declaration/dear-andy-burnham/
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R3 · P4 · culture_probe

What does Andy Burnham's government mean for repair and reuse in the UK? We're calling on him for policies to help us repair and save money. The post Dear Andy Burnham appeared first on The Restart Project .

### Hackers, Forklifts, and Other Things Manufacturers Blame on Repair
Source: Repair.org
Published: 2026-05-11 18:06 UTC
URL: https://www.repair.org/blog/2026/5/11/manufacturers-have-a-new-argument-against-repair-everyone-else-is-too-dangerous
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R3 · P4 · culture_probe

When lawmakers consider Right to Repair, manufacturers and their trade groups usually show up with the same script. They support repair, of course. They support consumer choice, naturally. They support small businesses, safety, sustainability, affordability, resilience, and probably puppies. They just oppose the actual law that would make repair possible. That script is on full display in Alaska, where HB 162 would give Alaskans more practical access to the parts, tools, and information needed to fix the products they own. And the opposition testimony has been revealing. Not because the arguments are new, but because they are so familiar, so exaggerated, and so openly hostile to the basic idea that anyone outside a manufacturer-controlled network can be trusted to repair anything. The message from industry is clear: repair is fine, as long as manufacturers get to decide who repairs, what gets repaired, when repair happens, and how much control they keep after the sale. That is not repair freedom. That is a service monopoly with better lighting.

### Cisco Is Hiding Its Repair Monopoly Behind “Security”
Source: Repair.org
Published: 2026-05-04 16:20 UTC
URL: https://www.repair.org/blog/2026/5/4/cisco-is-hiding-its-repair-monopoly-behind-security
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R3 · P4 · culture_probe

It took years of pressure, but consumers and advocates forced Apple to let people fix their own phones. That fight is not over. It just moved. Now the targets include everything from McFlurry machines to John Deere tractors to enterprise hardware from Cisco Systems. And right now, Cisco is leading the pack in the race to be the least repair-friendly major OEM. Over the past two weeks in Colorado, Cisco, IBM, and Hewlett-Packard Enterprise helped unleash more than 20 lobbying firms to kill the right to repair for businesses and government. Their argument was simple. If you do not use OEM parts, you are putting national security at risk. This is not just wrong. It is fiction. Since the first right to repair bill in 2014, not a single real-world example has been produced to support this claim. Not one. No breach. No incident. No evidence. Just hypotheticals dressed up as policy. Instead, we get scenarios that sound like action movies, not reality. They also tried to argue that repair should be restricted for equipment used in nuclear facilities and other sensitive environments.

### A Manufacturer’s Guide to Right to Repair Compliance
Source: Repair.org
Published: 2025-10-18 13:20 UTC
URL: https://www.repair.org/blog/2025/10/18/a-manufacturers-guide-to-right-to-repair-compliance
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R3 · P4 · culture_probe

Overview: The New Reality of Repair With Right to Repair laws now active in states representing over a third of the U.S. market, non-compliance is no longer a viable option. For manufacturers, this shift presents a critical choice: treat repair as a burdensome legal obligation or embrace it as a strategic opportunity to build customer loyalty, open new revenue streams, and enhance brand reputation. This guide provides a clear roadmap for navigating the new legal landscape. The Legal Foundation of Right to Repair Resistance to Right to Repair statutes has often been based on flawed legal arguments that have consistently proven ineffective. The legal framework supporting repair is built on long-standing principles: Basic Ownership Law (the First Sale Doctrine) : Many manufacturers have attempted to maintain control over products post-sale through End-User License Agreements (EULAs). However, legal precedent is clear: the transfer of ownership is complete at the point of sale . The new owner assumes responsibility for the product’s safety, security, and privacy.

### The Repair Association Backs Military Right to Repair (S.2209)
Source: Repair.org
Published: 2025-07-21 17:57 UTC
URL: https://www.repair.org/blog/2025/7/21/the-repair-association-backs-military-right-to-repair-s2209
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R3 · P4 · culture_probe

The fight for the Right to Repair has reached a critical milestone in Washington. The Senate Armed Services Committee is reviewing S.2209 , a groundbreaking bill that would guarantee our military’s right to repair the equipment they purchase and rely on. Today, we submitted a letter to Chairman Roger Wicker , Ranking Member Jack Reed , Senator Tim Sheehy , Senator Elizabeth Warren , and all members of the committee, voicing our full support for S.2209. This legislation would eliminate unnecessary repair monopolies, ensuring our armed forces can fix critical equipment on their own terms. Why S.2209 Matters Our military is the largest buyer of tech-enabled products in the world—yet too often, it’s locked out of essential repairs because manufacturers withhold access to parts, tools, or diagnostic information. These restrictions slow down readiness, inflate costs, and undermine the very concept of ownership. Repair isn’t about copying designs or exposing trade secrets. It’s a fundamental right tied to ownership, and it’s essential for operational readiness.

### Celebrating 50 States on the Right to Repair
Source: Repair.org
Published: 2025-02-24 10:00 UTC
URL: https://www.repair.org/blog/2025/2/24/ptkkw1yziw8xv7u9iwhoohl42ojyvu
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R3 · P4 · culture_probe

As of last week, all 50 states are now actively participating in the fight for our collective right to repair. Notably, Wisconsin and New Mexico introduced repair legislation for the first time, filling the final gaps on our nationwide map. Meanwhile, states like New York , Oregon , and Minnesota continue to enhance their existing laws, marking steady progress across the country. Our signature color scheme of black, red, and white has traditionally been used to signal clear legislative action. However, with many states now expanding their laws, we are exploring new ways to visually represent these developments without sacrificing clarity. Legislators are finding encouragement in the legislative successes of other states. Bills that pass in one state often pave the way for similar actions in others. By leveraging our experience from over 300 individual legislative efforts, we have been able to guide bill sponsors and drafters in avoiding early pitfalls and loopholes.

### John Deere Reaps What They Sow
Source: Repair.org
Published: 2025-01-21 15:19 UTC
URL: https://www.repair.org/blog/2025/1/21/john-deere-reaps-what-they-sow
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R3 · P4 · culture_probe

The recent news that the FTC and AGs from two farm states (IL and MN) have filed litigation against John Deere is a terrific step forward for Right to Repair laws already in effect or underway . Even the dissenting letters offered by two FTC commissioners support the Right to Repair but don’t like the timing of the filing, which is so close to a new administration in the White House. We understand the partisanship concerns, but there is no real excuse for a delay. John Deere has been dodging Right to Repair compliance for a decade in anticipation of actual enforcement being delayed or removed altogether. The FTC just laid down a marker that repair monopolies will be disbanded. Our organization, the Digital Right to Repair Coalition (aka repair.org) has been in the middle of this fight since our founding in 2013. We have worked tirelessly with legislators from both sides of the aisle to make sure that we can all fix our stuff. It’s our small “r” right to control the property we buy.
